# Autumn Budget 2025: descriptive replay

Positive GBP means a gain to the Exchequer. PolicyEngine is policyengine-uk 2.130.1 with policyengine 6.9.0, on certified data build policyengine-uk-data-1.58.1: one survey population whose data years are 2024–2032, projected by the engine to calendar year Y. Reform dictionaries use explicit 1 January windows for annual lookups (compute_uk_event.annual_reform). These calendar population inputs proxy OBR FY Y–(Y+1). OBR's announcement forecast and behavioural costings remain distinct from this construction.

Every source row remains visible: 1512 rows, 90 with computed counterparts. Source rows are counted once; no comparison subtotal is treated as a separately published OBR claim.

Net and absolute £ sum source-row values across the costing years; they are accounting amounts, not a single-year event total. Measure counts use each measure's registry class; source-row classes may differ for non-household heads.

### Not-expressible gap split

construction_pending names an unestablished replay construction; model_or_data_gap names an evidenced engine or data limitation. Both retain their not_expressible accounting class.

## Divergence axes and explained share

Every row tags population_vintage: OBR used the forecast at its fiscal event, whereas this release projects one later survey population. The other declared axes are behavioural_adjustment (static versus behavioural), baseline_vintage (certified versus announcement baseline), cy_proxies_fy and head_scope. Partial constructions also tag construction_scope.

The replay retains pinned incidence rules: employer_ni.employee_incidence=1 changes wages holding employer cost fixed, so its Income Tax effect is not a fixed-wage static costing. The OBR database reports direct measure effects and excludes separately reported indirect macroeconomic effects. Scope and incidence therefore require head_scope/construction_scope notes; neither a raw head gap nor the static label sizes a behavioural explanation.

Tagged coverage: 90 computed rows and £26.377bn of absolute raw gap have named axes. Tagged coverage does not size their effects.

Explained share: available on 0 computed rows. For other rows it is withheld because relevant axes remain unsized. A tagged limitation cannot claim a percentage of the gap. Residuals are therefore labelled residual_plus_unsized, not model error.

| Fuel Duty: Cancel uprating for 2026-27; extend the 5p cut in rates to 31 August 2026, then increase by 1p from 1 September 2026, 2p from 1 December 2026, and 2p from 1 March 2027 | 2025-26 | Fuel Duty | not_expressible / construction_pending | -0.047 | — | — | not_available | not_computed: The announcement is a within-year path (per the PMD title: the 5p cut extended to 31 August 2026, then 1p, 2p and 2p increases from 1 September 2026, 1 December 2026 and 1 March 2027). The replay executor takes only annual windows read at 1 January (pipeline/compute_uk_event.py annual_reform), which cannot express it, so an annual counterfactual is not guessed. #136 records a dated pre-Budget path from the OBR pre-measures forecast (ab2025__fuel_duty_freeze_extension_2026_27 in data/uk/ab2025_measures.json) for a later part-year construction. |

## Construction caveats

* `ab2025__personal_tax_thresholds_freeze_to_2031`: Authored against policyengine-uk 2.89.2: Reverses the PA/main/dividend HRT extension using the installed parameters' documented CPI uprating indices. The certified bundle already resumes uprating PT/UEL from 2028 and Class 4 limits from 2027, so it does not consistently encode the equivalent-NI freeze; this construction is partial and makes no NI threshold override. Construction from the OBR costings lane (data/uk/obr_measure_reforms.yaml, #56). Engine facts for this run are recorded in CONSTRUCTION_PREMISES.json.
* `ab2025__employer_nics_secondary_threshold_freeze_to_2031`: Authored against policyengine-uk 2.89.2: Counterfactual weekly values apply the installed parameter's documented OBR-CPI uprating to £96 from 2028. Exact source rounding and non-microsimulation heads are not assumption-matched, so this is partial. Construction from the OBR costings lane (data/uk/obr_measure_reforms.yaml, #56). Engine facts for this run are recorded in CONSTRUCTION_PREMISES.json.
* `ab2025__property_income_separate_rates`: Authored against policyengine-uk 2.89.2: Reverses the three certified property-income rates to 20%, 40%, and 45%. PE maps the direct Income Tax liability; the OBR also allocates effects to taxes and the Scottish block grant that this construction does not model. Construction from the OBR costings lane (data/uk/obr_measure_reforms.yaml, #56). Engine facts for this run are recorded in CONSTRUCTION_PREMISES.json.
* `ab2025__dividend_rates_plus_2pp`: Authored against policyengine-uk 2.89.2: Reverses the certified ordinary/basic and upper/higher dividend rates to 8.75% and 33.75%; the additional rate was not changed by this measure. PE maps Income Tax directly and retains the required broad NICs aggregate, but cross-tax, departmental-spending, and block-grant heads are outside this static construction. Construction from the OBR costings lane (data/uk/obr_measure_reforms.yaml, #56). Engine facts for this run are recorded in CONSTRUCTION_PREMISES.json.
* `ab2025__savings_rates_plus_2pp_and_starter_limit_held`: Authored against policyengine-uk 2.89.2: Reverses the three certified savings-income rates to 20%, 40%, and 45%. The same OBR measure freezes the Starting Rate for Savings limit at £5,000, but PE's parameter is constant at £5,000 since 2010 and carries no no-measure uprating path; that component is omitted. Construction from the OBR costings lane (data/uk/obr_measure_reforms.yaml, #56). Engine facts for this run are recorded in CONSTRUCTION_PREMISES.json.
* `ab2025__salary_sacrifice_pension_nics_cap_2000`: Authored against policyengine-uk 2.89.2: PE's exact pre-policy cap is infinity, which standard JSON cannot retain in the required run artifact. This construction uses a finite 1e100 cap, above any modeled contribution, and sets the model's fixed 0.16% broad-base pay-haircut response to zero; code inspection verifies that this leaves salary sacrifice uncapped on the modeled data. The certified formula otherwise embeds that behavioural incidence assumption, and the OBR scores small pre-2029 effects that the direct PE lever cannot produce. Construction from the OBR costings lane (data/uk/obr_measure_reforms.yaml, #56). Engine facts for this run are recorded in CONSTRUCTION_PREMISES.json.
* `ab2025__high_value_council_tax_surcharge`: Authored against policyengine-uk 2.89.2: Reverses the four surcharge band amounts to zero, as #136's registry does (ab2025__high_value_council_tax_surcharge in data/uk/ab2025_measures.json), from 2028, the PMD title's 1 April 2028 start. Engine facts for this run are recorded in CONSTRUCTION_PREMISES.json.
* `ab2025__winter_fuel_payment_income_test_35000`: Authored against policyengine-uk 2.89.2: Turns off the certified £35,000 England-and-Wales income passport while retaining the 2024 means-tested-benefit requirement. PE computes gross winter_fuel_allowance spending and Income Tax (expected to have no direct WFP recovery change); its formula uses income below £35,000 rather than the OBR description's below-or-equal wording and returns zero in Scotland. Construction from the OBR costings lane (data/uk/obr_measure_reforms.yaml, #56). Engine facts for this run are recorded in CONSTRUCTION_PREMISES.json.
* `ab2025__uc_child_element_remove_two_child_limit`: Authored against policyengine-uk 2.89.2: Reverses the UC child-element limit from infinity to two. The OBR also reports a Welfare outside cap row, but the harvest does not identify its program composition; CTC or devolved-offset mechanics are not assumed.
